### Settled

**Spec lane: retry outcome**
- **§7.3 is the failure-outcome home**: SPEC-1h edits §7.3 **Resume flow after pod failure**: step 2 "a reason on neither list is non-retryable"; step 4 "If retryable and retries exhausted → `awaiting_client_action`"; step 5 "non-retryable → `failed`", scoped to `running`, `input_required`, and `suspended`.
- **`resuming` failures**: §6.2 **`resuming` failure transitions** and the §7.2 `resuming → awaiting_client_action` edge own them; a non-retryable error during `resuming` goes to `awaiting_client_action`, which is why step 5 is scoped.
- **"unclassified" is not a spec term**: every staged "non-retryable or unclassified" became "non-retryable"; code's FailureUnknown already goes to `failed` from active states.
- **Edge triples**: SPEC-1a gives `running`, `input_required`, and `suspended` a triple mirroring §7.3 steps 3/4/5 (resume_pending: retryable and retries remain; awaiting_client_action: retryable and exhausted; failed: non-retryable), replacing the old unguarded `→ resume_pending` lines.
- **§6.2 copies**: SPEC-1d replaces both §6.2 `input_required` lines with "the three lines SPEC-1a gives"; SPEC-1i edits the §6.2 `suspended` block and the "Pod failure while `suspended`" paragraph.
- **Other SPEC-1 sites**: SPEC-1a also covers the §7.2 `input_required` paragraph; SPEC-1c the §6.2 maxSessionRetries bullet ("crashes always fail the session outright"); SPEC-1h the §7.3 **Expiry:** parenthetical.
- **Pointers, not copies**: SPEC-1b row, SPEC-1c bullet, SPEC-1d paragraph, the §7.2 `input_required` paragraph, and the SPEC-3 §28.8 cell point at §7.3; state-machine edge lines stay as edges.

- **SPEC-1e example**: the §8.8 "On failure:" TaskResult example keeps `"category": "TRANSIENT"` (the §16.3 category of a crash; CODE-4 is withdrawn) and flips `"retriesExhausted"` to false; the inserted paragraph cites §16.3 only, because §15.2.1 and §16.3 map no code and the §15.1 catalog has no RUNTIME_CRASH row. DOCS-1 item 8 flips the docs `classification` to `permanent` and `retriesExhausted` to false.

- **`perChildRetryBudget` is unimplemented**: children exhaust on their own RetryPolicy.MaxRetries; the only Go reference is the leasecontrol.go non-extendable guard.
- **Delegation awaiting state is bounded**: a child parked in `awaiting_client_action` expires after maxAwaitingClientActionSeconds, and Expiry applies cascadeOnFailure.
- **runtime_crash is retryable by default**: a stream-failure report goes to `resume_pending` under the default retryableFailures.
- **Gateway already routes exhausted to awaiting_client_action**: failure.go does it today, so the exhaustion outcome changes only in spec and docs. The one client-visible code change is the row outcome of a `POST /resume` that meets another replica's lease, as the **No client wire change** entry states.
- **No client wire change**: OpenAPI state and failureClass enums, SDK fields, and the §8.8 `awaiting_client_action` → MCP `input_required` mapping need no edit. A `POST /resume` whose bind meets another replica's lease keeps the 503 `RESUME_FAILED` envelope it answers today. CODE-2 item 8 changes only the row outcome, which stays in `awaiting_client_action` where today it goes to `failed`. The OpenAPI resume route declares only `200`, so it needs no edit.
- **client_only mode**: exists only in code and OpenAPI; it makes every listed reason non-retryable; absent from spec (pre-existing).

**Spec lane: stream rules and release**
- **SPEC-2a/2b card is the stream-rule home**: lifetime, failure predicate, report, discarded ends, and no-redial; the spec-changes **Design** numbered list was deleted.
- **Stream-failure predicate**: gateway did not end the stream and it ends with DEADLINE_EXCEEDED or INTERNAL; UNAVAILABLE, a clean close, or any other status is discarded. SPEC-2b and the SPEC-3 cell read the same.
- **DEADLINE_EXCEEDED is new normative text** in SPEC-2b; the code already uses codes.DeadlineExceeded on heartbeat miss.
- **SPEC-2b scoped release**: single-session pod released with the `failed` disposition; on a concurrent pod only the slot is released, and it counts toward the whole-pod replacement trigger (cites §5.2 **Failure isolation:**).
- **`failed` disposition**: the per-pod SandboxClaim terminal disposition (§4.6.1); it drains the whole pod; no slot-level disposition exists (adding one reverses 0079).
- **PodExecutor.Release**: ignores the disposition for a slot binding and calls `binder.ReleaseSlot`; CODE-2 may pass DispositionFailed on every edge.
- **SPEC-4 text**: names the gateway as the actor that counts the slot as `failed` toward the **Whole-pod replacement trigger:**, excludes only the other slot-retry-policy bullets, and names no emitter of `lenny_slot_failure_total`.

- **Gateway is the only emitter of `lenny_slot_failure_total`** in the tree (gwMetrics.IncSlotFailure via podBinder.SlotFailure); §5.2 sentence 1 names the adapter; recorded as a summary **Defects** bullet, F-5.2.13 is closed.

- **§28.8 preamble**: every cell derives from and cites a section or card, so a row restating its card is mandated.
- **Tenant pinning**: concurrent and recycling pools are tenant-pinned, so slot-only release does not weaken isolation.

**Adapter behavior (code facts relied on throughout)**
- **Runtime exit ends Attach cleanly**: a runtime connection end (crash, kill, or clean exit) closes the fan-out, Attach returns nil, and the gateway sees io.EOF with no report; only the next Attach ends INTERNAL (Output returns errRuntimeConnectionEnded). The adapter sends nothing on a crash.
- **Adapter status mapping**: heartbeat miss → DeadlineExceeded; runtime output or envelope-write error → Internal; unopened session or no runtime → FailedPrecondition; gateway end → Canceled; hold-state rejection → Unavailable; generation-stale and fence refusals → FailedPrecondition/Aborted.
- **Heartbeat is per Attach stream**: no stream means no heartbeat; a hung shared runtime ends every slot stream, staggered over up to one heartbeat interval; heartbeat frames carry no sessionId and reach every stream.
- **Adapter demux**: session-scoped frames are demultiplexed per stream by sessionId; an unaddressed one on a multi-slot pod is rejected.
- **runtimeForSession** returns the Runtime whenever the slot exists, so a write to a dead runtime maps to INTERNAL.
- **Close and Shutdown return quickly on a hung runtime** (socket Close is a no-op; InProcessRuntime.Close ignores ctx).
- **No adapter `RUNTIME_CRASH` synthesis**: covered by F-4.7.27 (scheduled under 0087) and a summary **Defects** bullet; RECORDS-1 files nothing new for it.
- **SIGSTOP checkpoint**: pkg/adapter/embeddedcheckpoint sends SIGSTOP but nothing imports it; recorded as a summary **Defects** bullet.
- **Adapter grpc server** sets no MaxConcurrentStreams; keepalive PermitWithoutStream=true; held idle streams cause no GOAWAY.

The summary's open section carries OD-12, OD-14, and OD-15.

**Non-spec lane: CODE-1 (reader and conn)**
- **Relay write error dropped** (CODE-1 item 5.1): the reader's next Recv supplies the status; gRPC SendMsg returns io.EOF when the server ended the stream (grpc v1.80.0).
- **endConn order** (CODE-1 item 6): classification and handler run before cancelling the conn and closing `done`.
- **Placeholder conn** (CODE-1 item 3): cache a placeholder under e.mu with a `ready` channel and open on a goroutine; Send waits on `ready` or its ctx; singleflight rejected.
- **Gate error fails closed** (CODE-1 item 5.1): relay a deny `tool_result` (`isError: true`, reason `approval_error`) through `conn.send`; nothing goes to the result channel, so each registration gets at most one write and capacity 1 is safe.
- **AwaitApproval returns**: error (no verdict) on gate not wired, session load, waiter register, interaction record, ctx end, poll error; deny on timeout (`approval_timeout`) and cancel (`cancelled`).
- **`--tool-approval-timeout` defaults to 0**: the gate arms no timer at 0.
- **e.mu**: streamFor takes executor-wide e.mu; grpc minConnectTimeout is 20 s, so an open under e.mu stalls the replica.

**Non-spec lane: CODE-2 and CODE-3**
- **ReportSessionFailure no-op on `resume_pending`** (CODE-2 item 1): moves StateResumePending into the no-op guard; item 1 names the applyFailureFromActive doc-comment update, and the CODE-2 tests rewrite TestReportSessionFailureDoesNotBumpCoordinationFromResumePending to assert no change and no release.
- **ReportSessionFailure has no production caller**: CODE-3 is the first; FailureReport.Reason is a string, so FailureRuntimeCrash needs string().
- **CODE-3 wiring**: in cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go after `sessionSrv := w.buildSessionServer(` beside `w.budgetTerminator.onTerminal`; buildSessionMessaging runs inside buildStores before the Server exists.
- **Report timeout**: a `sessionserver.Options` field set in sessionsrv.go beside `WorkspaceSealMaxDuration`; flag `--stream-failure-report-timeout` owes no docs row (no gateway-flag inventory exists).
- **Report context**: deadline = report timeout + `s.sealMaxDuration`. `sealWorkspace` has no `WithTimeout` in the tree (seal.go bounds its loop only with an `s.clock()` deadline); CODE-3 item 3.1 stages it.
- **Seal blocks**: the gateway Checkpointer has no Deadline and the seal loop checks its window only between attempts; recordSessionCompleted seals before release, child_failed, and cascade.
- **One-shot whole-pod trip** (CODE-2 item 6): `Trip` (check-and-mark under t.mu) replaces the `Unhealthy` check; `Tripped` is the query; a DrainSandbox error calls `Untrip`; `Forget` is unchanged and the mark survives it; a mark that survives the drain lives for the tracker's life; accountSlotFailure returns at entry for a tripped pod.
- **Sandbox names** come from GenerateName, so a tripped mark never matches a later pod.
- **DrainSandbox is idempotent**: the observable double count was `lenny_slot_pod_replacement_total`.
- **failure_test.go coverage**: retries-left, exhausted, and non-retryable from each of `running`, `input_required`, and `suspended`; the ordering bullet covers the `resume_pending` edge too.
- **Per-replica slot ledger**: slothealth.Tracker is in memory per replica; recorded as a summary **Defects** bullet plus a RECORDS-1 BUILD-GAPS item; the tier-5 concurrent case is pinned to one replica (Kind runs 2 gateway replicas).
- **BindResult.SlotID** is empty on exclusive pools, so `bind.SlotID != ""` separates slot accounting.
- **POST /resume admits only `awaiting_client_action`**: the release before a rebind runs in `handleResume` after that precondition check, so it cannot retire a held `suspended` pod.
- **Revocation is safe**: full_revoke cancels rows before Shutdown; ReportSessionFailure returns early on a terminal row.
- **Retry bound is gateway-owned**: RetryCount on the row, capped by retryPolicyCaps.

- **DOCS-1 label convention**: edge to `failed` reads `Non-retryable failure`; edge to `awaiting_client_action` reads `Retryable failure (retries exhausted)`; edge to `resume_pending` reads `Retryable failure (retries remain)`.
- **DOCS-1 item 3** (state-machines.md pod session-phase diagram): relabels the `attached` failure edges, adds `attached --> awaiting_client_action`, and gives the `suspended` failure edge its triple; the node already exists.
- **DOCS-1 item 9** (concepts.md): names the diagram edges, the **failed** and **awaiting_client_action** definitions, and the added running exhaustion edge; the sweep patterns are the DOCS-1 **Rules and sweep patterns** R1 to R7.

### Traps

**Spec lane**
- **Do not reintroduce "unclassified"** anywhere in the proposal; §7.3 step 2 defines a neither-list reason as non-retryable.
- **Do not unscope §7.3 step 5**: unscoped, it contradicts §6.2 `resuming` failure transitions, which send a non-retryable error during `resuming` to `awaiting_client_action`. Code splits applyFailureFromActive and applyFailureFromResuming.
- **Unguarded `→ resume_pending` edges overlap**: an edge guarded only by `retryCount < maxRetries` beside a `→ failed (non-retryable)` edge matches both for a non-retryable failure with retries left. Replace the old line with the guarded triple; never keep it and insert new edges after it. This bit the `suspended` line and later the `running` and `input_required` lines.
- **`starting → resume_pending` is deliberately unguarded**: the spec-changes "Launch-time retry paths" edge case scopes `starting` out. The §7.3 step 4 vs `starting → failed (retries exhausted)` tension predates 0091; do not fix or file it here.
- **MISTAKE: SPEC-1j pointing at the whole §7.3 flow**: round 2 cited the whole flow for the post-budget child state; §7.3 step 3 re-runs `retryCount < maxRetries` against the child's own maxRetries (default 2), so `perChildRetryBudget` (default 1) never decided exhaustion. Cite only step 4, and do not add "(perChildRetryBudget in place of maxRetries)". The budget-vs-child-maxRetries interaction stays unresolved (summary **Defects** bullet).
- **TaskResult and docs fields move together**: the spec example uses `category`, the docs Child Failure example uses `classification`; change both in the same pass. The PERMANENT example depends on code deriving category from §7.3 classification (errorclassify falls back to TRANSIENT; sessionrecord.RuntimeCrash hard-codes TRANSIENT), which is the code loop's job.
- **Do not re-add an emitter clause to SPEC-4**: and do not delete the CODE-2 item 5 step 3 counter increment or its tier-1 test; they use the existing gateway series and make the SPEC-3 observable true.
- **Do not rename the CODE-2 heading** "Release with the `failed` disposition": accurate for code (Release drops the disposition for slots).
- **SPEC-4 "changes no other text" scope**: worded "SPEC-4 changes no other text..." so it does not read as forbidding SPEC-1c/1d/1i edits to §6.2.
- **§28.6 SPEC-2c anchor**: the replaced sentence sits in **The second opener on those channels.** paragraph, between "The constraint excludes a second replica." and the Checkpoint/Interrupt clause joined by a semicolon; deleting it outright breaks that clause's lead-in.
- **Problem-statement lines are history**: "§28.6 sets no limit on CH-ATTACH streams" records the pre-fix tree; do not correct it.
- **§28.8 row restating the card is not a (g) copy**: the preamble mandates it; file only content the row adds beyond the card. The CODE-3 classification table citing SPEC-2b is not a copy either.
- **§5.2 **Failure isolation:** says nothing about releasing a slot's binding**: it covers marking `failed`, the metric, and the CH-ADAPTEREVENTS event; SPEC-2b states the release.
- **Adapter-synthesized RUNTIME_CRASH is a recurring lead**: settled as F-4.7.27; do not re-file under any lens.
- **Two failure budgets on concurrent pods are deliberate**: a crash goes through the adapter slot-failure event (§5.2 slot retry policy, fresh workspace, slotRetries); a hang goes through stream failure (§7.3, checkpoint restore, maxRetries).
- **retry lists are client-set**: "neither list → non-retryable" is the fail-closed direction.
- **DEADLINE_EXCEEDED lives only on the card and row**: §28.5.3 CH-MSGSOCK Timing says only "ends that session's stream".
- **Pre-existing, not filed**: §6.2 **Retry policy:** bullet lacks the "retryable" qualifier; the card's re-attach sentence restating §7.3 step 3b/3c is deliberate (spec-changes:16); the §5.2 whole-pod trigger aggregation across replicas predates 0091.
- **`*spec-changes.md` globs both files**: name spec-changes.md or non-spec-changes.md explicitly when reading.
- **Do not file usage.go emitChildFailed field names against DOCS-1 item 8**: the snake_case `retries_exhausted` vs docs camelCase and `error` object mismatch predates 0091; item 8 changes values only. The emitter's transient branch stays reachable via `starting → failed`.

**Non-spec lane**
- **Relay-write test polarity**: it lives under "Tests that land with CODE-3" and asserts the handler IS called once; a "no handler call" variant pins pre-fix behavior. Gateway-ended conns are TEST-1 (i).
- **Handler stays synchronous on the reader goroutine**: a goroutine reintroduces the race where a single-shot translator's deferred releaseSingleShot (Release → registry.Remove) beats ReportAttachStreamFailure's podRegistry.Get. A Send now waits up to the report timeout (30 s default) after a reportable end.
- **Duplicate-report guard**: the guard is the CODE-2 item 1 no-op on a `resume_pending` row plus the item 2 compare-and-swap (which deletes legalReportTransition); the window-expiry driver does not go through ReportSessionFailure. A client retry after a failed Send re-Attaches over the registered binding and can produce the second report this guard absorbs.
- **Failed open handling**: a failed open must close `ready` without starting the reader or closing `done`, and delete the entry only when it is still this conn; otherwise later Sends return the stale open error forever (TEST-1 (l), tier-7a open bullet).
- **No second result-channel write**: only `response` (and the stream-end completion) may write; a gate-error write blocks the single reader after Send leaves on ctx.Done, stopping Recv, endConn, and failure detection.
- **Seal bound must use WithTimeout**: `WithDeadline` on the `s.clock()`-derived deadline does not fire in tier-1 tests because s.clock is faked.
- **Report timeout covers everything but the seal**: if the store transition eats it, the post-seal release runs short; accepted as degenerate-datastore; do not add a second detached ctx.
- **accountSlotFailure is not "unchanged"**: accountFailedSlot and the trip text are in CODE-2 items 5 and 6; summary says "reuses its signature".
- **One-shot trip is shared code**: it changes slot-bind callers (applySlotRetryPolicy, re-attach); slotretry_test.go accountingFixture tests asserting a second drain after Forget need updating.
- **Tripped-pod entry check is not atomic with MarkLeaked**: a leaked failure passing the check just before a concurrent trip can re-raise the gauge; narrow, not staged.
- **Release-then-account order**: applySlotRetryPolicy releases before accounting, the opposite of CODE-2's order; neither order closes the recycle race (recycle path reads neither the drain annotation nor draining phase).
- **Approval wait on the session-scoped ctx**: with the default 0 timeout a pending approval blocks the single reader until resolution or release; do not claim it "matches today's behavior", and the flag help's "or the request context is cancelled" goes stale.
- **Runtime-kill tests need a follow-up message**: the kill yields io.EOF and no report; only the next delivery's INTERNAL produces the one `runtime_crash` report (TEST-2 tier 8 asserts this).
- **Do not re-file the 30 s cutoff of the failure-edge release**: Close and Shutdown return quickly on a hung runtime.
- **Sweeper connAlive uses Alive()**, which reads a post-drop IDLE channel as live, so after an UNAVAILABLE end the binding persists and the next message reopens.
- **Gateway never sets CoordinationGeneration on Attach frames**; in the shipped tree the Sweeper skips a bound session on `ErrHeld` without evicting, which CODE-5 changes.
- **SURFACE_OVERRIDES entries need `note`** as well as `surface` (KeyError otherwise), replace the whole surface, and abort the run when the key matches no row; the override must restate the adapter handler `pkg/adapter/attach.go` `Server.Attach`.
- **Do not credit 28.5.1 in spec-map yet**: it is excepted `pending-implementation` (blocker R16); new pod_test.go cases credit 7.2 and 7.3.
- **ScrubReport may record a stream-failed slot as leaked again**: stricter, not weaker; not filed.

## Ledger

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: a DrainSandbox error at the one-shot trip calls the existing `Tracker.Forget`, extended to clear the trip mark, so the pod's next threshold crossing re-drains — BECAUSE Forget's only production caller (slot_bind.go accountSlotFailure) is the call CODE-2 item 5.2 replaces, so reusing it restores today's recovery without a new method — ALTERNATIVES: new `Untrip` method (orphans Forget); trip only after a successful drain (two concurrent callers would both drain and replace); "next accounted failure re-drains" (false: the trip zeroed events and leaks).
WATCHOUT: do not move the trip after DrainSandbox; the concurrent one-shot guarantee needs the trip taken under the tracker mutex before the drain — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 5.2
FACT: DrainSandbox is a single podclaim.StampDrainRequest merge patch with no retry, and accountSlotFailure only logs its error — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:189-196; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:99-106

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a failed DrainSandbox at the CODE-2 trip calls the existing `slothealth.Tracker.Forget`, extended to also delete the trip mark; the next ceil(n/2) threshold crossing re-trips and re-stamps the drain — BECAUSE this restores today's recovery (Forget after drain, slot_bind.go accountSlotFailure) with no new tracker method, and Forget's only caller is the call CODE-2 item 5.2 replaces — ALTERNATIVES: a new Untrip method (second method beside an orphaned Forget); trip only after a successful drain (lets two concurrent callers both drain, reopening the race the one-shot trip closes); skipping the replacement callback on drain error (changes today's behavior, unreviewed scope).
WATCHOUT: the reviewer's suggested wording "the pod's next accounted failure re-attempts the drain" is false: the trip already cleared the pod's events and leaks, so the retry happens at the next threshold crossing, as with today's Forget. ### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: `DefaultWorkspaceSealMaxDuration` is 300 s, so CODE-3 item 3.1's report deadline defaults to 330 s (30 s report timeout plus the seal window). A `Send` waiting on an ended conn is still bounded by its own request ctx, so the longer deadline stalls only the reader goroutine. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:25, sessionserver.go:1606-1608
FACT: today `sealWorkspace` bounds its window only through the `s.clock`-based `deadline` it checks between attempts (seal.go:113-140). The `context.WithTimeout` that CODE-3 item 3.1 describes is new code that CODE-3 adds, which is why seal.go is in its Targets. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:100-150
FACT: `PodExecutor.Release` never waits on a conn's `done`. It calls EvictStream (takes e.mu), then registry.Remove, then the binder. So a handler that runs synchronously in endConn and reaches Release cannot deadlock with the reader, provided endConn releases e.mu after step 2. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:312-328
FACT: `adapterclient.Attach` opens without WaitForReady (`c.rpc.Attach(ctx)` followed by a first Send). On a dead adapter the async open in CODE-1 item 3 fails once gRPC's connect timeout expires, so the placeholder conn does not hang forever even though its ctx is detached. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:871-883
DECISION: no finding on the one-shot trip's lifetime-long tripped set. BECAUSE it gains one entry per whole-pod trip (a rare event), and the tracker's `leaked` map already keeps per-pod entries for pods that never trip. ALTERNATIVES: filing it as unbounded memory growth; rejected because the growth is a few bytes per pod replacement.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: stale-read duplicate report closes by making each transition helper's already-in-target closure branch return errReportConflict (transitionToResumePending, transitionToAwaitingClientAction, transitionToFailed), with "committed transition" defined once in CODE-2 item 1 as a return whose To is the helper's target state; items 3 and 4 point at item 1 — BECAUSE ReportSessionFailure's guard read (store.Get) sits outside the Update closure, and the nil branch makes the helper return From=running To=target for a no-op write — ALTERNATIVES: a `wrote` field on FailureDisposition (duplicates the bit errReportConflict carries); moving the guard read inside the closure (restructures the funnel); comparing disp.From != disp.To (that is exactly what passes for the stale duplicate).
FACT: the conflict path returns To=row.State (the stale snapshot state), so a stale duplicate from `resuming` yields To=resuming and applyFailureFromResuming skips the coordination bump; the first report's bump stands — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go applyFailureFromResuming (`disp.From == StateResuming && disp.To != StateResuming`), transitionTo* conflict branches.
DECISION: accountFailedSlot takes the reason as an explicit fourth argument (`rep.Reason`) — BECAUSE the helpers return a FailureDisposition rather than the updated row, and `row` is the pre-transition snapshot whose FailureReason is stale — ALTERNATIVES: pass `rep` (only the reason is needed); return the updated row (three signature changes).
WATCHOUT: the sequential two-report test was replaced by the stale-snapshot case; the sequential case is item 1's resume_pending guard, already pinned by the rewritten TestReportSessionFailureDoesNotBumpCoordinationFromResumePending. Do not re-add it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Tests that land with CODE-2.

DECISION: "commits a real transition" is closed in code rather than prose: each transition helper's already-in-target closure branch (transitionToResumePending `r.State == StateResumePending`, transitionToAwaitingClientAction `r.State == StateAwaitingClientAction`, transitionToFailed `session.IsTerminal(r.State)`) returns errReportConflict instead of nil; CODE-2 item 1 is the one home and defines a committed transition as a helper return whose `To` is the helper's target state; items 3 and 4 point at item 1 — BECAUSE with item 1's ReportSessionFailure guard those branches are reachable only by a stale read, so converting them makes the disposition decidable and also stops the duplicate recordSessionRetry, emitAwaitingClientActionEntered, and recordSessionCompleted side effects — ALTERNATIVES: a "wrote" flag set in the closure and returned on FailureDisposition (new field, more surface for the same bit); moving the SELECT-FOR-UPDATE guard read into ReportSessionFailure (restructures the funnel); qualifying the prose again (failed in rounds 1-3).

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
- FACT: The adapter's runtime-output hub broadcasts serially and blocks on a subscriber whose 64-frame feed is full, so a gateway reader that stops calling Recv can, once gRPC flow control fills, stall sibling slots' output (including heartbeat_ack) on a concurrent pod — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/linefanout/linefanout.go:36 (feedDepth = 64), :121-131 (broadcast loops s.send), :251-256 (send blocks until done). Only the approval-gate wait can stop the CODE-1 reader; it is pre-existing and accepted in the Edge cases bullet, and needs a chatty runtime, so not filed.
- FACT: The adapter exit path calls grpc GracefulStop with no bound; with CODE-1's held Attach streams, an adapter SIGTERM while a stream is held waits for the stream to end (runtime exit ends it with a clean EOF, which is discarded). Not filed: the bound is the pod's grace period and the spec's one-long-lived-stream rule already implies it — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/exit.go:29-37.
- FACT: CODE-2/CODE-3 add no Redis dependency and no etcd writes beyond the existing claim retire and drain annotation. The report does one Postgres Get+Update per stream end that is classified a failure, on context.Background bounded by 30 s + sealMaxDuration (300 s default, seal.go:25). A report that fails while Postgres fails over leaves the binding in place, and the next message re-escalates, which still does better than the shipped tree (it never reports) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:115-160.
- FACT: Tracker.Forget has exactly one caller (slot_bind.go:196), so extending it to clear the trip mark affects only the CODE-2 drain-error path.
- USEFUL [standing context "Adapter grpc server sets no MaxConcurrentStreams"]: settled the question of whether held streams per pod hit a stream cap.

USEFUL [Settled: Plan item]: the standing-context entry matched the plan exactly; nothing new for this lens.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
FACT: pgstore Update runs the closure under SELECT ... FOR UPDATE and writes the row back even when the closure returns nil without mutating, so transitionToResumePending/transitionToAwaitingClientAction return To=<target> (From=the caller's stale read) to a concurrent duplicate whose closure hit the already-in-target branch — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:400-404; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:240-243, 270-272, 255-258
DECISION: filed the long-carried OPEN "commits a real transition undefined" as a finding — BECAUSE a concurrent Send opening conn C after endConn step 2 evicted conn B can produce a second INTERNAL report that reads `running` before B's commit; CODE-2 item 4 then accounts the slot twice (premature ceil(n/2) drain) — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it OPEN (it has been carried since non-spec.1 and never filed)
FACT: the path-6 resume-and-deliver fallback buffers a failed Send into the inbox using the stale `row`, and bufferIncomingMessages never re-reads state; after CODE-3 the handler commits resume_pending (and runs migrateInboxOnResumePending) before Send returns, so the fallback writes into an in-memory inbox that was already drained to the DLQ — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:206-213; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_inbox.go:51-56; non-spec-changes.md:43; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:310-316, :346
FACT: a release failure on the new resume_pending edge leaves the claim held until the session reaches a terminal state, because orphan GC only reclaims claims whose pod has no non-terminal session; bounded by resume window + awaiting window, so not filed — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:78-85
FACT: gateway->adapter client keepalive is configured (10 s / 5 s defaults), so a partitioned held stream ends UNAVAILABLE rather than hanging the reader — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:211-217, flags.go:1073-1078

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
FACT: slotstate.MarkLeaked is accounting only (leak gauge and RecordLeak input); the Redis slot counter, not MarkLeaked, governs slot reuse, so CODE-2's tripped-pod early return before MarkLeaked does not make a leaked slot reassignable — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:170-180; pkg/sandbox/slotstate/registry.go:93-98
FACT: the drain-request stamped at the one-shot trip is consumed by the WarmPoolController regardless of the idle label, so a successfully tripped pod does leave service and the in-memory trip mark is not a relaxed bound (a replica restart only clears it, which can re-drain, the stricter direction) — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/pod_reconciler.go:589-596; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:835-836
FACT: resumeOnPod always claims a fresh pod (startOnPod / whole-sequence Bind), so CODE-2 item 6's release-first with DispositionFailed never retires the pod it then binds — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:35-71
FACT: sealWorkspace on a cut-short seal returns lastErr with outcome timeout, and the caller emits the workspaceSealFailed audit event, so the CODE-3 item 3.1 context bound surfaces rather than hides a degraded seal — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:100-150
DECISION: security lens returns no finding in round 4 — BECAUSE the approval gate stays mandatory and fail-closed on every reader path (CODE-1 item 5.1), the retry bound is the gateway-owned RetryCount, stream-failure classification is the gateway's reading of adapter gRPC status (the runtime can only crash or hang its own session), slot-only release stays inside tenant-pinned concurrent pools, and the stale-replica approval path is OD-3 — ALTERNATIVES: filing the stale-replica approval answer (rejected: open decision OD-3)

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the reliability lens in spec round 1 — BECAUSE the two recovery defects this lens reached were already filed this round by other lenses (the §10.1.4 whole-pod connection-loss reaction vs SPEC-2b's UNAVAILABLE no-report text, filed by review-mechanism; the SPEC-1c **Retry exhaustion:** bullet and the §6.2 trailing "the crashed pod is drained" paragraph staying unscoped after SPEC-4h, filed by another lens), and every other lead is settled or pre-existing — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing both duplicates (rejected: costs four verifiers and closes nothing new).
FACT: a replica-crash between the session transition and the binding release leaves a single-session pod claimed; the reclaimer is the adapter hold timeout (coordinatorHoldTimeoutSeconds 120 s, which closes CH-MSGSOCK and retires the pod) plus the orphan session reconciler, so SPEC-2b's "on that transition the replica releases" order needs no extra reclaimer. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58-59
FACT: a failed stream-failure report (for example Postgres down) is self-healing: the binding stays, the next message delivery opens a new Attach, and the heartbeat miss or the INTERNAL end produces the report again; a hang with no further message falls to the session watchdog. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2b, edge case "Hang before the first message"
WATCHOUT: a transient partition (>15 s) between a live lease-holding replica and its pod puts the adapter in hold state; the holder's next Attach is rejected UNAVAILABLE/coordinator_hold, which SPEC-2b discards with the binding kept, and no path lets the holder re-fence, so the session dies at the hold timeout. Pre-existing §10.1.4 gap (hold exits only on a fence from a "new coordinator"); not filed against 0091. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:57-58

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: Security lens found no regression of an established control in the staged spec edits. Concurrent pools are always tenant-pinned and cross-tenant slot sharing is rejected at validation (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:562), so SPEC-4's slot-only release on a concurrent pool does not open cross-tenant reuse; maxConcurrentSessions: 1 pools keep the `failed`-disposition retirement (SPEC-2b, SPEC-4b). — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:458,562
FACT: Every bound the stream-failure path feeds is gateway-owned: retryCount is on the Postgres row; the adapter's gRPC status only selects report vs discard, and a pod-induced report can at most consume the session's own retries or retire its own pod. The whole-pod trigger ledger is in-memory per replica (pre-existing, summary Defects bullet); SPEC-4 adds an input to it, not a new durability dependency. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:604
WATCHOUT: Stale-replica reports (a fenced replica's held stream ending DEADLINE_EXCEEDED) are OD-3 territory (per-frame coordination_generation defect, CODE-5 mitigation); do not re-file under security. — EVIDENCE: summary.md OD-3 entry

### [spec.5.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings in round 5 — BECAUSE no staged edit gives the agent pod a new action or apiserver path. The retry bound (RetryCount on the session row) and the §7.3 classification are gateway-owned, and "neither list → non-retryable" fails closed. SPEC-4's concurrent-pool carve-out keeps tenant pinning (§5.2 **Tenant pinning:** requires a pin when maxConcurrentSessions > 1). §7.3 step 4 already sent exhaustion to awaiting_client_action before 0091 — ALTERNATIVES: filing the per-replica in-memory whole-pod trigger as a non-durable security bound (rejected: it predates 0091, the edit does not change its storage, and the summary already lists it as a Defect).
FACT: a §7.3 resume of a concurrent-pool session onto the same pod is guarded by the adapter's slot-identifier reclaim hold, because the slot id equals the session id. A slot whose cleanup failed holds the id for the pod's life, so dropping the §5.2 "Fresh workspace guarantee" bullet for stream-failed slots (SPEC-4a "in place of the other slot retry policy bullets") opens no inheritance path — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:396, :464-467, :478

Retirement of a stream-failed single-session pod is still stated, through SPEC-2b's `failed`-disposition release. Do not file this as a dropped one-session-only control unless SPEC-2b loses that clause — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:186-188, :269

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: CODE-5 item 1 gates the `ErrHeld` eviction on a Sweeper-local map of the `coordination_generation` last renewed at (guarded by `s.mu` beside `backoffUntil`); evict only when the listed row's generation exceeds the recorded one — BECAUSE `leasestore.Failover` keeps unreconciled Redis and Postgres holder rows, so `ErrHeld` can name a stale Redis holder after a sub-TTL Redis outage and evict the current coordinator — ALTERNATIVES: a generation field on `BindResult` (changes every `BindingRegistry` implementer, contradicts item 5); a gRPC probe of the pod's fenced generation (an RPC per bound session per sweep); consulting the Postgres fallback or mirror row (neither is authoritative); withdrawing CODE-5 (OD-3 is settled).
WATCHOUT: on the takeover edge, record the `RecordHandoff` return value. `row` is the pre-bump `List` snapshot, so recording `row.CoordinationGeneration` there makes the new coordinator evict itself on its next `ErrHeld`. Treating a missing map entry as generation 0 reintroduces the wedge. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go `Sweep` takeover branch (`generation := s.RecordHandoff(...)`, then `s.upsertMirror(..., row.CoordinationGeneration)`)
WATCHOUT: the tier-1 test's unbound third session must hold a `rep-2` lease on the first sweep too. With no lease, a running-pod row is `adoptable`, and `rep-1` takes it over on the first sweep. — EVIDENCE: coordination.go `adoptable := leaseUnheld && isRunningPod(row) && ...`
FACT: `coordination_generation` is also bumped on non-terminal edges by the session's own coordinator: `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose` runs on resuming → resume_pending/awaiting_client_action (failure.go `applyFailureFromResuming` tail; resume.go resume-error path). The design's "only RecordHandoff advances a non-terminal row" is wrong. It is harmless for CODE-5 because those edges release the binding, so `bound` is false. Summary decision 15 says "which a peer's takeover bumps", without "only". — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:219, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go:91
FACT: the tier-8 crash-takeover subtest runs `coordinator.Sweeper.Sweep` at generation 1 before the survivor's takeover, so replica-1 records 1 and still evicts at 2; it needs no edit. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/coordination_crash_takeover_test.go:133-148
FACT: dual-store residual. After a Redis outage, the stale replica whose Redis lease is still live renews successfully and records the peer's advanced generation, so it never evicts. The edge-case bullet **Stale coordinator between a peer's adoption and its own next sweep** names this residual, and the per-frame CH-ATTACH check (RECORDS-1 item 7) would close it. A separate pre-existing defect: the failover.go doc claims that "the authoritative holder re-establishes in Redis". Not filed here, per the design.
CORRECTS [standing context, Open]: removed the entries that later rounds closed. Exported-identifier claim: no "only new exported" text remains. Suspended edges in docs: DOCS-1 items at non-spec:160-185. docs/api/internal.md: DOCS-1 edits 10 and 11. state-machines.md:252: DOCS-1 item 4. Tier-11 card/row naming: the test no longer names them. Heartbeat-pages DEFERRED: DOCS-1 R3 plus the retired phrases in `TestRetiredFailureOutcomePhrasesAbsent`. Updated the Settled OD-3 line to the gated trigger.
OPEN: routing of the persistent standing-context entries. The following stay open for the next non-spec fixer: `resume_rebind_test.go` is not in the tree but Files touched does not mark it "(new)" (non-spec:373), a one-word fix; the mcptools.go:1856 DEFERRED (no deliverable stages the comment); "committed transition" in CODE-2 item 4 is still undefined against `transitionToResumePending`'s no-op return; Send's return on its own `conn.send` failure. The rest (0081 impact row, two retry budgets, emitter BUILD-GAPS, client_only, emitChildFailed comment, and the UNVERIFIEDs) are summary-level or out-of-scope questions for the open-decisions pass, and they stay recorded.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 evicts on `ErrHeld` only when the bound session's row `coordination_generation` exceeds the generation this Sweeper last recorded for it (recorded on each successful Acquire of a bound session, or the RecordHandoff result on the takeover edge after publish; cleared in evictBinding and the terminal-row branch; guarded by s.mu beside backoffUntil). No entry keeps today's skip. — BECAUSE leasestore.Failover keeps independent Redis and Postgres holder rows with no reconciliation, so ErrHeld can name a stale holder after a Redis outage shorter than the TTL; the generation is authoritative. — ALTERNATIVES: a generation field on BindResult read through BindingRegistry (rejected: interface change across four implementers and every bind-and-publish site); probing the pod's fenced generation per sweep (rejected: an RPC per bound session per sweep); dropping CODE-5 (rejected: OD-3 owner decision).
FACT: for a non-terminal row only the Sweeper's RecordHandoff advances coordination_generation; the session server's bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose runs on terminal edges, and Sweep skips terminal rows. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:463-480, :276-282; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_generation.go:39; callers failure.go:219, resume.go:91, session_transitions.go:49,:99
FACT: BindResult carries no generation; bind sites never record one. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindrequest.go:130
FACT: the tier-8 subtest "survivor adopts lapsed lease..." already runs a successful replica-1 Sweep at generation 1 before the crash, so it records 1 and still evicts under the gate; it needs no change. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/coordination_crash_takeover_test.go:129-151
WATCHOUT: on the takeover edge record `generation` from RecordHandoff, not row.CoordinationGeneration: the row is the pre-bump List snapshot, and recording it would let the next ErrHeld evict the new coordinator. — EVIDENCE: coordination.go:370-430
WATCHOUT: residual the gate does not close (pre-existing Failover divergence, not CODE-5): after the outage the stale replica A, unbound with priorHolder == A, self-renews its Redis lease forever and upserts the mirror as coordinator; the failover.go doc claim that "the authoritative holder re-establishes in Redis on the next pass" is false in that case. Separate finding, not this group. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:27-32; coordination.go:335, :370

### [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the SPEC-1e insert and DOCS-1 item 8 both say the child "lists `runtime_crash` under `nonRetryableFailures`, so the child failed on its first crash". BECAUSE `ClassifyFailure` keeps the default `retryableFailures`, which contains `runtime_crash`, when only `nonRetryableFailures` is overridden, and it checks the retryable list first, so that policy classifies the crash as retryable. The summary's OD-11 recommendation already depends on this. ALTERNATIVES: also changing the spec to define list precedence (rejected: adds a spec rule; rewording the example is enough).
FACT: `resumeOnPod` has a second caller besides POST /resume: `sessionNodeReattacher.ReattachNode` in treerecovery.go. It runs only for nodes with no registry entry (`nodeNeedsRecovery`), so CODE-2 item 7's release-first is a no-op there. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/treerecovery.go:28-29, :71-77
FACT: `messagerouting.Classify` sends `resuming` to ActionDeliver (path 2), because `isRecovering` covers only resume_pending and awaiting_client_action. A Send can therefore open a stream while the row is `resuming`, and a report against that row takes `applyFailureFromResuming`, which does no release. The window is the gap between `resumeOnPod`'s Put and `transitionResume`, so I did not file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/messagerouting/messagerouting.go:94-100, :149-176; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go:105-108
CORRECTS [Standing context Traps "Do not touch legalReportTransition"]: CODE-2 item 2 now deletes `legalReportTransition` and replaces it with a snapshot-state compare-and-swap. Only `ReportSessionFailure` calls the helpers, and the window-expiry driver does not, so the trap is stale. Retire it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:178-216 are the only helper call sites
WATCHOUT: a stale replica whose stream ends with DEADLINE_EXCEEDED or INTERNAL before CODE-5's next sweep still reports, because the registry entry is present until EvictBinding runs. ReportSessionFailure's compare-and-swap is on state, not on coordination_generation, so the stale replica writes the row and releases the pod that the peer coordinates. The outcome matches what the peer would produce for a hung runtime, so I did not file it. It is not named in the OD-3 residual. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:227-240; non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 item 5
USEFUL [Standing context "Sweeper connAlive uses Alive()"]: saved tracing the UNAVAILABLE end through the Sweeper.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: ToolApprovalGate.AwaitApproval polls the Postgres interaction store every 25 ms (one pgtenant.InTx read per tick) for the whole wait; shipped Send runs on r.Context(), so the poll ended when the client's HTTP request ended. CODE-1 item 5.1 moves the gate to the session-scoped conn context, so with --tool-approval-timeout 0 the poll lasts until resolution or binding release (idle clock defaults to maxSessionAge, 2 h). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/toolapproval.go:27, :146-160; pkg/gateway/session/interactionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:89-95; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:146; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:283
FACT: CODE-5 eviction writes nothing to the lease store, the mirror, or Postgres; after a Redis reset the adoption race (peer sees ErrNotFound, adopts, fences) is shipped behavior and CODE-5 only adds the stale replica's eviction, which is no worse than the shipped skip. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:299-341; pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:67-76
FACT: Held streams at the Tier 3/4 budget (400 sessions per replica) cost about 3 goroutines each on the gateway; no per-replica stream budget exists in the spec, and the adapter fan-out queues 64 frames per subscriber. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:77-82; pkg/adapter/linefanout/linefanout.go:35-37
WATCHOUT: a Postgres failover now auto-denies every pending (long-lived) approval with approval_error on the first failed poll; this is the settled fail-closed design, not a separate finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/toolapproval.go:150-154

FACT: Sweeper interval defaults to 15 s and TTL to 4x interval (60 s). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:179-188
WATCHOUT: CODE-5's premise "ErrHeld on a bound session means a peer adopted and fenced" (non-spec-changes.md:126) is false across a Redis outage shorter than the TTL. A peer that sweeps first during the outage finds no Postgres row, adopts a live session, and fences it (coordination.go:288-302, 335-346, 370-399). After Redis recovers, that peer's Acquire returns ErrHeld against the original holder's unexpired Redis lease, so CODE-5 evicts the coordinator that holds the current generation. The authoritative discriminator is the Postgres row's coordination_generation (sessionstore.go:325), which a handoff always bumps. EVIDENCE: as above
FACT: The message path has no on-demand re-adopt. An unbound running session fails Send with "not bound to a pod" and buffers to the inbox. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:136-139
OPEN: After the CODE-2 failure-edge release, the reporting replica's Sweeper keeps renewing the lease through the priorHolder==self branch while it holds no binding. A POST /resume that lands on another replica then fails closed on ErrHeld (resume_rebind.go:51-58, 107-115). This also holds before 0091 for awaiting_client_action rows that the watchdog enters, so it is not filed here. A later proposal should decide whether the failure-edge release also releases the coordination lease.
FACT: treerecovery.ReattachNode is a second caller of resumeOnPod. It reaches resumeOnPod only for nodes that have no registry entry, so the release-first in CODE-2 item 7 is a no-op there. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/treerecovery.go:28-31, 69-76

DECISION: security lens returned no findings — BECAUSE the bounds the proposal touches (RetryCount on the Postgres row, the lease compare in Acquire, the tenant read from the binding) all come from trusted components, a runtime-induced INTERNAL end only tightens outcomes (retry spend, slot counted toward drain), and concurrent-pool non-retirement rests on tenant pinning (settled) — ALTERNATIVES: filing CODE-5's missing `coordinator_preempted` log and `lenny_coordinator_handoff_stale_total` increment was rejected, because §10.1.5 item 4 triggers on a generation-stale rejection, not on a sweep `ErrHeld`

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
FACT: Held CH-ATTACH streams cost one gateway reader goroutine plus one adapter heartbeat loop per bound session; at Tier 3 that is up to 400 per replica (`maxSessionsPerReplica`), and heartbeat traffic is ~10,000/30 s ≈ 333 frames/s fleet-wide. Neither is a budgeted resource. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:85, cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:225-227
FACT: linefanout blocks the hub's single reader once one subscriber's 64-frame feed is full, so a stalled consumer on a concurrent pod can stall its sibling slots. Reaching that needs the gateway reader parked in the approval gate and more than one gRPC flow-control window of that session's own frames, because the demux drops other sessions' frames without blocking. Judged hypothetical; not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/linefanout/linefanout.go:35-38, :232-237
FACT: the watchdog's resume_pending→awaiting_client_action sweep releases no executor binding; only the CODE-2 funnel releases on the failure edge. An ambiguous Postgres commit (committed, error returned) leaves the row in resume_pending with the binding held, and a repeat report is a no-op under CODE-2 item 1. The shipped tree never releases either, so this is no regression; not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:719-747
FACT: CODE-5 adds no Redis or Postgres call to Sweep, which already lists every session per tenant (ListFilter{}). The generation map is bounded by bound sessions and is cleaned up on the terminal and unbound branches. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:271-346

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
FACT: The stream's open context `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` carries the first request's context values, but nothing downstream reads identity from them: `adapterclient.Client.Attach` attaches no per-RPC metadata, `ToolApprovalGate.AwaitApproval` takes the approver from `row.UserID`, and pgstore takes the tenant as an explicit argument. A carried-over principal therefore cannot be attributed to a later turn. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:871-883, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/toolapproval.go:84,158, pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:7-12
FACT: The slotstate `MarkLeaked` feeds only the leaked-slot gauge. Occupancy authority lives in the Redis slot counter, so CODE-2's tripped-pod early return, which skips `MarkLeaked`, does not let a leaked slot be reassigned. EVIDENCE: pkg/sandbox/slotstate/registry.go:7-12,93-98
FACT: In the shipped `accountSlotFailure`, a failed `DrainSandbox` is logged and then runs `Forget`/`ForgetPod` anyway. CODE-2's `Untrip`-on-error is therefore stricter than the tree and is not a regression. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:190-203
DECISION: Under the security lens I returned no findings. BECAUSE: CODE-5 eviction is gated on the Postgres `coordination_generation`, which is a trusted and durable value, and it writes no lease. The approval gate fails closed and relays nothing on an evicted conn. The retry bound is the gateway-owned `RetryCount`. Slot-only release keeps isolation because concurrent and recycling pools are tenant-pinned (spec/05 tenant pinning). The per-replica slot ledger predates this proposal and is already filed. ALTERNATIVES: I considered and rejected (a) a tier-9 coverage finding for the approval-gate change, because tier 1 cases (j), (k), and (m) pin the fail-closed paths and approval is not a tier-9 surface in the tree (no tier9 test references it); and (b) a CAS-conflict no-op leaving a hung runtime to recycle, because that is a same-tenant liveness outcome rather than a security-control regression.

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: The Sweeper renews a lease this replica holds for any non-terminal row, bound or not (`eligible := bound || priorHolder == s.replicaID || adoptable`), and `adoptable` needs `isRunningPod` (running/input_required with a pod), so a lease left on the reporting replica after a resume_pending/awaiting_client_action edge is renewed until the row goes terminal; once released it is not re-adopted. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:240-242, :335
FACT: A resume on a replica that does not hold the lease fails with leasestore.ErrHeld in resumeOnPod (registerBinding / acquireCoordinationLease), and holdOrFailOnResumeError demotes it to `failed` because isTransientPodClaimError has no ErrHeld case. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:51-58,107-115; resume.go:77-91,192-203
DECISION: Close the stranded-lease finding with one clause in CODE-2 item 4's resume_pending/awaiting_client_action bullet: after releaseExecutor, call the existing s.releaseCoordinationLease(ctx, row.TenantID, row.ID) and name this caller in its doc comment; plus one tier-1 and one tier-4 test bullet. — BECAUSE the helper is holder-checked, best-effort, and already the co-location rollback (§4.6.1); no new surface. — ALTERNATIVES: tightening Sweeper eligibility to `priorHolder == s.replicaID && isRunningPod(row)` (rejected: lease still blocks other replicas for the 60s TTL, and it changes CODE-5's package for a lease-holder bug the funnel creates); classifying ErrHeld as transient in isTransientPodClaimError (rejected: hides a live foreign coordinator; resume must fail closed against double-bind, resume_rebind.go:51-58 comment).
WATCHOUT: Order matters: release the lease AFTER releaseExecutor. Released first, `bound` is still true and the next Sweep re-acquires the lease on this replica. — EVIDENCE: coordination.go:296,335
WATCHOUT: A Sweep whose Get read priorHolder == this replica just before the funnel's Release can still re-Acquire the unheld lease in the Get-to-Acquire window and then renew it indefinitely. The existing rollback callers (create_and_start.go:509, delegated_child_materialize.go:186) have the same window. Not staged here; a candidate separate finding against the Sweeper's priorHolder-renew branch.
OPEN: Is the same strand present on other non-terminal edges that drop a binding without releasing the lease (e.g. suspend/pod-release paths into resume_pending)? Not checked; this proposal only owns the failure funnel.

### [non-spec.6.review-performance.1]
FACT: Held CH-ATTACH streams at the top tier cost one gateway reader goroutine plus one adapter Attach handler, recv loop, demux, heartbeat monitor, and linefanout subscriber pump per bound session. maxSessionsPerReplica is 400 at Tier 3 and Tier 4, so that is at most 400 held streams per replica. The proposal adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write per message. Writes happen only on a stream failure: one session-row CAS, the existing retry and inbox records, and at most one drain-annotation patch per tripped pod. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:78-85; spec/16_observability.md:601
FACT: linefanout.broadcast sends to subscribers one after another. A subscriber whose 64-deep feed is full blocks the shared reader for every sibling slot. The package comment says a slow subscriber blocks "only its own pump", which this contradicts. It does not affect 0091, because the adapter Attach loop keeps draining `out` unless gateway HTTP/2 flow control stalls its stream.Send. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/linefanout/linefanout.go:34-38 (feedDepth), :118-128 (broadcast)
FACT: Full-level quiesce runs on the runtime's CH-RUNTIMEOPS background loop. Heartbeat acks continue during a CheckpointBarrier, so gateway drain does not trip held-stream heartbeat escalations. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1937-1951
FACT: The adapter's SIGTERM path calls GracefulStop. srv.Serve then returns and main exits, so held streams do not stall pod exit. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:476-491; pkg/adapter/exit.go:29-37
FACT: resumeOnPod's other caller is the §8.10 tree reattacher. It reaches resumeOnPod only for a node with no podRegistry entry, so CODE-2 item 7's release-first is a no-op there. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/treerecovery.go:28-29, :71-77
WATCHOUT: Between a peer's takeover and the stale replica's next sweep, the stale replica's held stream can itself end with DEADLINE_EXCEEDED. ReportAttachStreamFailure checks only the local podRegistry, not the lease, and so can commit the §7.3 transition and release from a non-coordinating replica. The coordinator's own report then loses the CAS. This falls inside the accepted "Stale coordinator between a peer's adoption and its own next sweep" window, and the outcome state is the same. Do not file it unless that edge case is reopened. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:115-140 (no lease check); non-spec-changes.md:371

On an unreachable coordinator it fails closed", each 1 hit. Do not shorten them. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:569-571

### [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the §29.3/§7.2 lease-holder contradiction by keeping the failure-edge lease release and conditioning the §29.3 terminate (DELETE inherits) and POST /resume rows on whether another replica holds REG-COORDLEASE, giving the release one spec home as a clause in SPEC-2b, and deleting "on the coordinating replica" from the §7.2 durable-inbox Per-message TTL row — BECAUSE the tree has no forward for /resume and the spec names no carrier, so a lease kept on the failed replica makes every off-holder resume fail closed — ALTERNATIVES: drop SPEC-1l and the failure-edge lease release (rejected: resume then succeeds only on the holder replica, the ownership invariant inverts, residual 2 becomes the normal path, and CODE-2 items 4/7/8 plus decision 9 all need rewriting); moving the two states into the no-holder clause unconditionally (rejected: residuals 2 and 3 leave a live holder in awaiting_client_action, and CODE-2 item 8's refusal needs the foreign-holder branch); naming the gateway leader as trimmer owner (rejected: this invents a placement, the tree's sibling DLQ sweep runs on every replica's watchdog, and the durable trimmer is unbuilt).
FACT: in the shipped tree the failure funnel releases neither the binding nor the lease, and the Sweeper renews an unbound lease forever via `priorHolder == s.replicaID`, so today a recovering session keeps its coordinator until the row goes terminal. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go (no releaseExecutor or releaseCoordinationLease); pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:335
FACT: the durable-inbox per-message TTL trimmer (reason durable_inbox_ttl_expired) is unbuilt. Only the reason constant exists. The DLQ TTL sweep runs on every replica's watchdog, keyed on Postgres state, not on the lease. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/events.go:30; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:674-697; cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:66
WATCHOUT: the SPEC-2b lease clause must exclude the `failed` edge. The non-spec ownership table lets a terminal session's lease lapse at its TTL, so an unscoped "releases the binding and the lease" contradicts it. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md ownership table, `failed` edge row
WATCHOUT: the §29.3 tool-use/elicitation rows ("Any non-terminal state", forward) already name states with no holder (created, ready). The recovering states join them under this fix. This is pre-existing imprecision and is not filed here.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S1 says "Lands the mid-session failure-outcome reconciliation and the §29.6 restore precondition, all of SPEC-1". After the fix SPEC-1l also edits the §29.3 terminate and resume rows and the §7.2 Per-message TTL row, so the step description should read "and the coordination-lease edits of SPEC-1l" (or name §29.3 and §7.2 too).

### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
FACT: the staged spec adds no per-message store write. Writes occur only on a stream failure: one session-row CAS, the existing retry and inbox records, a holder-checked lease release, and at most one drain-annotation patch per tripped pod. Held CH-ATTACH streams are bounded by bound sessions per replica (maxSessionsPerReplica 400 at the top tiers). No etcd, hot-key, or informer concern at the top tier — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:78-85 (tier table); spec/28_communication-channels.md:137 (REG-COORDLEASE is Redis CAS, 60 s expiry)
FACT: a report whose Postgres CAS fails during a failover is logged and not retried. The stream is already evicted and the binding is kept, so the next message delivery's Attach re-detects a dead or hung runtime. The shipped tree never reports at all, so this is no regression — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 3.4
WATCHOUT: the §29.3 **Off-holder matrix.** terminate and DELETE rows say the spec "establishes a coordinating replica holding REG-COORDLEASE" in `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action`, and the resume row requires a forward to that coordinator. Redesign 1 releases the lease on the failure edge (non-spec ownership table) and SPEC-1l moves §29.6 to "acquires ... when it binds". The redesign reconciled only the resume row's live-foreign-holder case. Filed this round as a spec finding — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:569-571

### [redesign1.apply.1]
- FACT: this pass applied redesign 1 (scratchpad/redesign/0091_fix_write-the-gateway-stream-failure-proposal-that-gateway-runti-redesign-1.md, edits E1 to E53 with E21a). Every anchor matched once at the point its edit ran, each paragraph-range start preceded its end, and no edit was skipped. Two corrections followed the edits: the SPEC-1m staged text said "E7's state-column replacement", an identifier internal to the redesign, and now says "SPEC-1l's state-column replacement"; and the summary Spec bullet and the SPEC-2 deliverable-index line, which E17 and E25 left ungrammatical, were rejoined.
- DECISION: three areas were redesigned. (1) The `REG-COORDLEASE` lifecycle is stated once, in a new §10.1.1 **Lease lifecycle:** bullet (SPEC-1m), which SPEC-1l, SPEC-2b, SPEC-2g (`CH-ATTACH` **Exclusivity.**), and SPEC-2h (§28.3 `REG-COORDLEASE` row) cite; SPEC-1m also corrects the §29.3 terminate, start, and finalize rows and §29.2 step 11, which said the specification gives no acquisition point. CODE-2 item 10 makes the Sweeper renew a self-held lease in `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` only while bound. (2) A resume whose bind meets another replica's lease keeps the 503 `RESUME_FAILED` answer, and `holdOrFailOnResumeError` writes `awaiting_client_action` only over a `resuming` row (CODE-2 item 8); SPEC-1n names the cause in the §15.1 catalog row and §29.6 step 4, DOCS-1 edit 23 and rule R8 carry it to `docs/reference/error-catalog.md`, and RECORDS-1 item 10 files the missing §29.3 forward. (3) Summary decision 9 states once where each release runs, and the two Watch-out bullets that restated the ownership guards were deleted — BECAUSE spec.6.review-applicability.1 and spec.6.review-edit-sites.1 found that §29.3 still stated a coordinator in both recovering states, spec.6.review-client-surface.1 and spec.6.review-mechanism.1 found that the `coordinatingReplica` key had no spec home, and spec.6.review-fresh.1 found that CODE-2 item 8 applied the unreachable-coordinator refusal to a live holder — ALTERNATIVES: OD-R2 option (b), below.
- FACT: the redesign deleted the coordination-lease release in CODE-2 item 7 (`releaseEarlierBinding` no longer calls `s.releaseCoordinationLease`), the `TARGET_NOT_READY` answer to `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/resume` on `ErrHeld`, the `coordinatingReplica` details key, the `s.leaseStore.Get` read in `handleResume`, the test name `TestResumeReleasesEarlierBindingAndLeaseBeforeBind_spec_10_1` and its "no holder after a failed bind" assertion, `TestResumeAgainstForeignLeaseHolderHoldsRow_spec_7_3` (replaced by the `_spec_15_1` case and `TestHoldOrFailOnResumeErrorKeepsNewerState_spec_7_3`), the two summary Watch-out bullets on funnel order and the resume release, and the Traps bullet **Replaced test**. It added `TestSweepDoesNotRenewUnboundRecoveringLease_spec_10_1_1` under the CODE-2 tests, `coordination.go` to the CODE-2 Targets and deliverable index, the dependency S10 on S7, and renumbered RECORDS-1 items 10 to 12 as 11 to 13.
- FACT: the sweep for the replaced design removed three statements of what no longer exists: the CODE-2 item 8 clause "adds no `writePodClaimError` case, no `s.leaseStore.Get`, and no details key", the CODE-2 item 7 sentence "It releases no lease." (the ownership table's Release-first Lease cell states it), and "and no lease release" in `TestSlotAccountLockContextCancel_spec_5_2`. ### [redesign1.apply.2]

RECORDS-1 item 10 files three facts as one finding: the recovering-state lease lifecycle, the missing §29.3 resume forward, and the resume bind's missing §10.1.2 compare-and-swap. TEST-2 (d) now asserts the off-holder resume holds the row with 503 `RESUME_FAILED` and the holder's resume succeeds.

### [spec.7.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1m start acquisition reads "when it starts the session on its pod, before it commits the session to `running`"; §29.3 rows say "when it starts the session"; §29.2 step 11 says "The replica that starts the session at step 21 acquires" — BECAUSE spec "bind" is the creation-time `bound` claim state (§29.2 step 7), and every tree start path acquires before the running commit — ALTERNATIVES: a route list (misses the delegated-child path, goes stale); "start-time bind" (still collides with step 7).
FACT: start paths acquire the lease before the `running` commit at three sites — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_registration.go:36 (via start.go:87), create_and_start.go:493, delegated_child_materialize.go:152; create.go acquires nothing.
DECISION: SPEC-1m adoption point is "when it adopts a session's lapsed lease" with no state list — BECAUSE §29.7, the §29.8 intro, §10.1.7 (spec/10:126), and §10.1.4 hold state need a peer to take over `starting`/`suspended` sessions; the later "No replica adopts ... `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action`" sentence is the one home of the exclusion, and "lapsed" (holder stops renewing) excludes never-held `created`/`ready`/`finalizing` — ALTERNATIVES: repeating the exclusion in the clause (double statement); explicit state list (stale); keeping the narrowing (spec would terminate pod-held suspended sessions on every rolling update).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce "with a pod assignment" as the adoption predicate; a `created` row already carries pod_assignment — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.3 finalize row.
FACT: the tree's narrower adoption set is filed as RECORDS-1 item 11 (isRunningPod); RECORDS-1 closures/plan items renumbered 12-14, no citer named their numbers — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:240.
FACT: standing-context audit retired the "(new) mark" Open (non-spec-changes Files touched now marks resume_rebind_test.go new) and the §10.1.4 UNVERIFIED (RECORDS-1 item 8 and a Defects bullet file it), and updated the Open-decisions entry (summary section reads "None.").

### [spec.7.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1n's §29.6 step 4 replacement says the session row "returns to `awaiting_client_action` unless another write has moved it on", with no source state — BECAUSE §29.6 step 3 and the §7.2 edge `resume_pending → resuming` enter `resuming` only once a pod is allocated, so a pool or credential exhaustion allocates no pod, and §6.2 **`resuming` failure transitions** (the authoritative enumeration of edges out of `resuming`) has no transient-claim edge — ALTERNATIVES: adding a `resuming → awaiting_client_action` transient edge to §6.2 and §7.2 (writes the tree's order into the spec), or re-defining step 3 so `resuming` starts at admission (out of scope; the divergence predates 0091).
WATCHOUT: do not put a source state back into the SPEC-1n step 4 text. The tree's `resuming`-guarded hold (handleResume writes StateResuming before resumeOnPod) lives in CODE-2 item 8 and the release-first row of non-spec-changes, and those describe code correctly — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.6 step 3 ("moves on to the internal `resuming` state when a pod is allocated"); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go handleResume.
OPEN: the tree enters `resuming` before it claims a pod, while §29.6 step 3 and §7.2 enter it on allocation. This tree-vs-spec ordering divergence predates 0091 and is unfiled. A later round should decide whether to file it as a finding.
Standing-context audit (resume-outcome entries only): no Settled or Traps entry states a source state for the SPEC-1n outcome, so none needed correction.

### [spec.7.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1l's §7.2 **Per-message TTL** edit now replaces the trim sentence with one Redis script that evaluates each head entry's `enqueued_at` + `per_message_ttl` itself, emits `message_expired` once per removed entry, and stages the §12.4 inbox key row's "TTL trim via `LRANGE`+`LTRIM`" as "TTL trim via one atomic head-trim script" — BECAUSE with no lease holder in the recovering states (SPEC-1m) several replicas can trim, and a count-based LRANGE+LTRIM run twice drops unexpired messages, breaking the §7.2 durable-mode no-drop guarantee — ALTERNATIVES: a leader-elected single evaluator (adds an election and reintroduces a single runner); keeping the coordinating replica as runner (no holder exists there); deleting the §12.4 clause (leaves the key's operation list incomplete); a staged clause defending concurrency safety (reviewer-facing).
WATCHOUT: do not stage a script that takes a count or range computed by a Go-side LRANGE, and do not name a runner (each replica, leader, watchdog) in the staged text; the predicate must be evaluated inside the script, and atomicity is what makes the runner irrelevant — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md Per-message TTL row; spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.4 inbox key row; DLQ sibling is one script `sweepExpiredScript` in pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go.
CORRECTS [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]: its reliance on "run it like the DLQ sweep on every replica's watchdog" was unsafe for the inbox as staged, because the DLQ sweep is safe only as a single Lua script while the inbox row still said LRANGE+LTRIM; the staged text now makes the inbox trim atomic too.
FACT: the §12.4 inbox key row is the only other spec site naming the inbox LTRIM (grep `LTRIM` spec/ hits §7.2 and §12.4 only) — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:188.

- WATCHOUT: "lapsed" is defined by the bullet itself ("A lease its holder no longer renews lapses at its expiry"), so a `created`, `finalizing`, or `ready` session, which no replica has held, has no lapsed lease and is not adopted. Do not drop the state list in favour of pod_assignment in code: a `created` row already carries pod_assignment — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.3 finalize row; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go isRunningPod doc comment

### [spec.7.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1n §29.6 step 4 replacement drops "from `resuming` " and keeps "unless another write has moved it on" — BECAUSE §29.6 step 3 and §7.2 (`resume_pending → resuming (pod allocated ...)`) put a pod-less session in `resume_pending`, and §6.2 **`resuming` failure transitions** is the authoritative edge list out of `resuming`; spec text must not encode the tree's write order — ALTERNATIVES: adding a §6.2/§7.2 `resuming → awaiting_client_action` transient-claim edge (rejected: grows the state machine to match a tree ordering the spec does not state; hair).

### [spec.7.review-mechanism.1]
- FACT: the shipped Sweeper adopts a lapsed lease only for `running`/`input_required` rows with a pod assignment (isRunningPod), so a pod-held `suspended` or a `starting` session whose coordinator dies is never adopted. SPEC-1m writes that predicate into §10.1.1 as the exhaustive list of acquisition points, which contradicts §10.1.7 **Cold-start behaviour after rolling updates.** ("every inherited session triggers a coordinator handoff on its new replica") and the §10.1.4 hold state that waits for a new coordinator. Filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:233-241; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:126, :56-58
- FACT: `handleResume` writes `resuming` before it claims a pod (resume.go:69-76), but §29.6 step 3 says the row enters `resuming` only "when a pod is allocated", and §6.2 **`resuming` failure transitions** is the authoritative list of edges out of `resuming` and has no transient-claim-failure edge. SPEC-1n's "returns from `resuming`" in step 4 encodes the code order against both. Filed. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.6 steps 3-4; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:235-241
- WATCHOUT: the `created` session row already carries `pod_assignment` (§29.3 finalize row reads it), so "a session with a pod assignment" is not enough to make it adoptable. Any widening of the SPEC-1m adoption clause has to name the states (`starting`, `running`, `input_required`, `suspended`) rather than key on `pod_assignment` alone. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:573
- FACT: the tool-use/elicitation resolution routes record the interaction phase even when no executor is blocked (toolapproval.ErrNotFound "the interaction phase is still updated"), so the SPEC-1l "records the resolution in that durable state" insert matches the tree. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/toolapproval/toolapproval.go:26-30

### [spec.7.review-performance.1]
- FACT: the §7.2 DLQ TTL sweep is safe to run on every replica because it is one Lua script (ZRANGEBYSCORE plus removal in `sweepExpiredScript`), driven from each replica's watchdog over Postgres-listed recovering rows. The durable-inbox per-message trimmer the spec states is two commands (`LRANGE` + `LTRIM` by count), so it is not safe to run on more than one replica at once. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:142-143; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:673-697; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:299
- WATCHOUT: SPEC-1l deletes " on the coordinating replica" from the §7.2 **Per-message TTL** row so the trimmer can run with no lease holder. That leaves several replicas able to trim one list concurrently, and a trimmer can race the resuming replica's delivery and `LREM`. A count-based `LTRIM` run twice, or run after a head `LREM`, drops unexpired messages and emits `message_expired` twice. Filed this round. The fix is to make the trim atomic (one Redis script that removes only the entries whose own TTL has passed), not to name an owner. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:184; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:299, :301-302
- FACT: SPEC-1m's adoption predicate (`running` or `input_required` with a pod assignment) matches the Sweeper's `isRunningPod`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:241, :302
- FACT: at the top tier the staged spec adds no store write per message. Lease renew volume goes down, because recovering sessions with no binding are not renewed. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:78-83
- USEFUL [spec.6.review-performance.1]: its write-rate FACT still holds for the SPEC-1m/1n additions, so I did not redo the math.

### [spec.7.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: SPEC-1l's deletion of " on the coordinating replica" from the §7.2 durable-inbox **Per-message TTL** row leaves a non-atomic `LRANGE` + `LTRIM` head trim with no single runner. Two evaluators that read the same expired head each `LTRIM` by their own count, so the second one also drops messages that have not expired, and each one emits `message_expired`. §12 Redis key table row `...:inbox` repeats "TTL trim via `LRANGE`+`LTRIM`". Filed. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:184; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:299; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:188
- FACT: Sweeper adoption (`isRunningPod`) is running/input_required with a pod assignment, which matches SPEC-1m. Suspended-with-pod sessions are never adopted in the tree either, so SPEC-1m's adoption scope is code-faithful. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:240-242,301
- UNVERIFIED (non-spec loop): the Sweeper evaluates `isRunningPod` on its List snapshot. A failure edge that commits `resume_pending` and then releases the lease between that List and the Sweeper's `Get` lets a peer adopt a recovering session: `RecordHandoff` refuses only terminal rows, so the peer fences and binds, and it then renews while bound. The adoption predicate needs a re-read of the row state at adoption time. This is code, so it was not filed here. — EVIDENCE: coordination.go:288-301,363-372
- WATCHOUT: the OD-1 residual ("every exit from `awaiting_client_action` drains while the coordinator lives") was reasoned before the failure-edge lease release. The replica that keeps a default-mode in-memory inbox after the direct `running → awaiting_client_action` edge no longer coordinates the session. OD-1 is settled, so this was not filed, but a non-spec reviewer should confirm what happens to that in-memory inbox on release.
