### [spec.9.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed no findings on the staged spec edits under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE no staged edit adds an etcd, Postgres, or Redis write per unit of work beyond the shipped crash-retirement path, and every failure mode traced (Postgres failover during a report, Redis reset, coordinator handoff, gateway drain) degrades no worse than the shipped tree, where a stream failure was never reported at all — ALTERNATIVES: filing the held-stream count at Tier 3 (400 sessions x 30 replicas) was rejected, because §4.1 sets maxSessionsPerReplica by an empirical ramp test and states no per-stream budget.
FACT: the Sweeper already renews a self-held lease with no binding (`eligible := bound || priorHolder == s.replicaID || adoptable`), and it adopts lapsed leases only for running/input_required rows with a pod (isRunningPod). So a session parked in awaiting_client_action after a stream failure becomes resumable from any replica once its holder dies, and the lease column adds no new Redis write class — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:238-240, :327
FACT: a stream-failure report lost to a Postgres failover leaves the row `running` with its binding in place. The next delivery's Attach re-detects the failure: INTERNAL right away for a dead runtime, or DEADLINE_EXCEEDED after 10 s for a hung one. With no further message, nothing re-reports, and that matches the shipped tree's zero detection — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:556-557 (CH-MSGSOCK Timing)
WATCHOUT: `lenny_stream_proxy_p99_attach_latency_seconds` (§4.1 calibration and extraction trigger) measures client session-attach latency rather than CH-ATTACH opens, so held streams do not change its sample rate. Do not file it — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:114, spec/04_system-components.md:89-90

### [spec.9.review-reliability.1]
- DECISION: no spec-lane findings under the reliability lens in round 9 — BECAUSE the spec staging is unchanged since round 8 (no spec.8.fix entries), and every recovery path traced holds: coordinator crash between the failure commit and the binding release is reclaimed by the §10.1.4 hold timeout (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58); the `resume_pending` window expiry runs in the watchdog without a lease gate (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:719); the gateway's own shutdown cancel ends held streams with a status SPEC-2b discards; the new §7.3 step 5 leaves step 3c's "same as step 4" pointer valid (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:404-415) — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing SIGSTOP vs heartbeat (summary Defects) or the kept-lease RESUME_FAILED loop (owner decision); both settled.

- OPEN: non-spec loop, CODE-3 item 3.2 guards a report only by the local registry. In the window between a peer takeover and this replica's next Sweep (CODE-5 eviction), a stale replica still holds a cached stream when the per-frame CH-ATTACH generation check is absent (OD-3 defect). A runtime hang ends both replicas' streams with DEADLINE_EXCEEDED. If the stale report commits `resume_pending` first, the coordinator's report is a no-op (CODE-2 item 1), so the coordinator never releases its live binding to the hung pod. SPEC-2b says only "the coordinating replica reports", so the spec is right; whether CODE-3 should also compare the row's `coordination_generation` against the binding's generation before reporting is a non-spec question. Unfiled.

CODE-3 makes stream failures lead to awaiting_client_action rows that hold leases, because no driver exists. — ALTERNATIVES: leave it to RECORDS-1 item 10 (rejected: item 10 states the claim but not the retire or the multiplier, and it closes nothing). The suggested fix is a lease Get before the claim, with no lease write.

### [non-spec.10.review-performance.1]
FACT: The preStop CheckpointBarrier target set comes from the coordination_lease mirror, which the Sweeper upserts for every held lease, including the self-held unbound leases that 0091 leaves in resume_pending and awaiting_client_action. PodDispatcher.resolve and Checkpointer.snapshot both fail fast with no binding, so those targets add no barrier wait. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:585-611, pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/wiring.go:63-69, pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:423-426
FACT: No production code reads the REG-COORDLEASE holder for routing outside Sweep (leases.Get is called only at coordination.go:289). After a Redis reset, a self-held unbound recovering-state lease is not re-established, because Sweep's eligibility is bound || priorHolder==self || adoptable and isRunningPod excludes the recovering states. Resume becomes more permissive (any replica acquires), which is no worse than the shipped tree. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:240-242,335
FACT: Shipped holdOrFailOnResumeError and isTransientPodClaimError have a single caller chain (handleResume at resume.go:92), and the handleResume failure exit bumps coordination_generation through bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose. A misrouted resume therefore bumps the generation, but CODE-5 records the generation only for sessions that are bound here, and only an ErrHeld from another holder evicts, so the bump does not falsely evict a holder that is renewing. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume.go:77-99
DECISION: no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens. BECAUSE the new etcd writes are bounded: one claim release per failure edge, and at most one drain annotation per pod under the one-shot Trip. The new in-memory state is per session or per drained pod, and the Redis reset, Postgres failover, and coordinator handoff paths degrade no worse than the shipped tree, which kept the binding and renewed the same lease. ALTERNATIVES: filing the unbounded growth of the tripped-pod mark map. Rejected because it gains one entry per drained concurrent pod, which the failure rate limits, so it is not material.

### [non-spec.10.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens for CODE-2 items 4, 7, 8, 9, the ownership table, CODE-5, or the reduced-scope tests — BECAUSE every recovery claim checked against the tree holds: the Sweeper self-held renew (`eligible := bound || priorHolder == s.replicaID || adoptable`) keeps the lease in the recovering states; resumeOnPod returns ErrHeld unwrapped after rollbackBinding on both branches; the hold guard matches the SessionStore.Update abort contract; the leaked-pod cases in residuals 1 and 2 are bounded by the claim GC (bound claims drained after the 5 min orphan timeout); CODE-5's tier-8 extension works because replica-1 renews at generation 1 before the lapse — ALTERNATIVES: filing a request-context release in releaseEarlierBinding (rejected: rollbackBinding on the same path already uses r.Context(), and claim GC bounds the strand); filing the funnel-lock hold across recordSessionCompleted on the `failed` edge (rejected: no other path takes that session's lock once the row is terminal).
FACT: SessionUnrecoverableMarker (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/adapters.go:137-149) moves a non-terminal root, including a bound `running` one, straight to awaiting_client_action. So CODE-2 item 7's release-first can retire a live, healthy binding on the next POST /resume. Before 0091 the new Put overwrote it and leaked the pod to claim GC, so the release is an improvement rather than a defect — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/adapters.go:142
FACT: the orphan session reconciler's eligible set excludes resume_pending, awaiting_client_action, and resuming (pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:67-72). The funnel's retire of a single-session pod therefore does not make the session orphan-eligible after the commit.
UNVERIFIED: failOrphan's mutator checks only IsTerminal and does not re-check eligibility (orphansession.go:342-349). A tick that listed the row as `running` before the funnel commit, and read the pod as terminated after the funnel retired it, would write `failed` over `resume_pending`. The window is the list-to-row iteration time against the pod's drain-to-terminated time, so I judged it below the bar. A later reliability or Kubernetes lens could measure the drain-to-terminated latency.
USEFUL [non-spec test-coverage DECISION at review-log:1769]: it had already rejected the untested "release error is logged and the resume proceeds" branch of item 7, so I did not re-file it.
